#2913. Top Wall Street analysts recommend these 3 dividend stocks for higher returns

Dividend-paying stocks can enhance portfolio returns by providing a steady income stream to investors and the potential for capital appreciation. Several investors seek exposure to dividend stocks in a volatile backdrop to ensure stable income.

In this regard, recommendations of top Wall Street analysts can help shortlist attractive dividend stocks, backed by strong financials and cash flows to support consistent dividend payments.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Energy Transfer (ET) is a diversified midstream energy company with about 140,000 miles of pipelines and associated energy infrastructure in 44 states. The company made a quarterly cash distribution of 34 cents per common unit for the second quarter of 2026. At an annualized payment of $1.36 per common unit, ET offers a yield of 6.3%.

Recently, JPMorgan analyst Jeremy Tonet reiterated a buy rating on Energy Transfer stock and slightly raised his price target to $25 from $24. The analyst noted that the company delivered a strong Q2 beat across all key metrics.

Tonet highlighted that Energy Transfer raised its 2026 adjusted EBITDA guidance to $18.8 billion-$19.1 billion from $18.2 billion-$18.6 billion, reflecting robust base results and cost optimization. The 5-star analyst added that the company "tightened" its capex to $5.6 billion–$5.9 billion and expects capex above $5 billion annually through 2029, given solid opportunities.

About the Hugh Brinson pipeline, Tonet noted Phase 1 (1.5 bcfd) reaching full capacity by September 1 and Phase II capacity (0.7 bcfd) going online by Q1 2027. The analyst also noted completion of a 14-mile Hugh Brinson lateral in Abilene, Texas, and final stages of talks for an additional 250 mmcfd of Oklahoma power plant demand.

"All in, we see ET well positioned to continue capitalizing on organic growth opportunities across the value chain," said Tonet.

Tonet ranks No. 922 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 9%. See Energy Transfer Financials on TipRanks.

This week's second dividend pick is independent oil and natural gas company Permian Resources (PR). The company declared a base dividend of $0.16 per share for the third quarter, payable on September 30, 2026. At an annualized dividend of $0.64 per share, PR offers a yield of about 2.7%.

Recently, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on Permian Resources stock and increased his price target to $27 from $22, citing an improved outlook on oil production growth and operational efficiencies.

The 5-star analyst noted that while PR stock has outperformed its peers so far in 2026, he sees further upside. Mehta is confident about the company's operational improvements and ability to deliver incremental free cash flow per share through the strategic ground game program. He added that the program has completed about $1.05 billion in bolt-on deals this year (as of August 5).

"We see the continued success of PR's ground game as constructive toward future inventory replenishment and increasing working interest over time," said Mehta.

The analyst also emphasized Permian Resources' solid track record of generating growth in free cash flow (FCF) per share. Mehta expects the company's free cash flow per share to grow at a 20% CAGR (compound annual growth rate) from 2025–2028.

Mehta ranks No. 401 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 12.8%. See Permian Resources Statistics on TipRanks.

Moving on to Sempra Energy (SRE), a utility holding company. Earlier this month, the company declared a quarterly dividend of $0.6575 per share, payable on October 15. At an annualized dividend of $2.63 per share, SRE stock offers a yield of about 3.1%.

Recently, Jefferies analyst Julien Dumoulin-Smith upgraded SRE stock to Buy from Hold but slightly lowered the price target to $97 from $101. The 5-star analyst noted that Sempra stock is trading at a price/earnings multiple that is 14% below its electric peers due to concerns about Texas transmission capital expenditure and the failure of California legislation.

Smith noted that the derating of weaker California peers PG&E (PCG) and Edison International (EIX) is weighing on the sum-of-the-parts (SOTP) valuation but is becoming less relevant given their materially higher fire risk compared to Sempra Energy.

Furthermore, Smith expects Sempra Energy's Texas capex plan to stay in place despite the opposition to transmission and data centers, though delays to 765-kV projects are likely. While a full recovery for SRE stock will need a clearer outcome from the Texas legislative session, Smith believes that the stock may already be an early opportunity for patient investors.

"We are early on the upgrade but see limited downside at current levels as the market is pricing in little transmission upside," said Smith.

Smith ranks No. 945 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 61% of the time, delivering an average return of 8.2%. See Sempra ETF Exposure on TipRanks.

#2902. Federal Reserve Board releases results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 10:00 a.m. EDT Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Friday released the results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families.

The data provide a representative picture of what Americans own, how and how much they borrow, and how they bank. The current version of the survey has been undertaken every three years since 1989 and provides the public with an extensive time series of thorough information on the economic conditions of American families. Alongside the survey data, the Board issued its summary report entitled Changes in U.S. Family Finances from 2022 to 2025 (PDF), which examines changes in income, net worth, assets, debt, and financial vulnerability.

Key findings from the 2025 survey include:

In addition to the summary report, an interactive chartbook displaying changes in these financial characteristics over time is available here. Additional data from the survey, including extensive data examining other economic conditions of American families, is made available to researchers and the public here.

The survey is conducted for the Board by NORC, a social science research organization at the University of Chicago. Participants in the study are chosen at random from 119 geographic areas, including metropolitan areas and rural counties across the United States, using scientific sampling procedures that aim to produce a sample representative of American families.

#2893. Top Wall Street analysts find these 3 stocks attractive as long-term investments

Investors have been grappling with stock market volatility, weighing elevated oil prices, persistent geopolitical tensions, the Federal Reserve's rate hike and a debate about AI safety. For all that, it helps to look beyond short-term noise and focus on stocks with strong long-term growth potential.

Top Wall Street analysts' recommendations can help investors gain clarity, as these experts conduct in-depth research to pick stocks with attractive growth prospects.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Enterprise software and cloud infrastructure company Oracle (ORCL) recently announced better-than-expected results for the first quarter of fiscal 2027. The quarter benefited from solid growth in cloud infrastructure revenue.

After hosting a series of investor meetings with Ken Bond, Oracle head of investor relations following Q1 earnings, Guggenheim analyst John DiFucci reiterated a buy rating on the stock with a price target of $400. Oracle remains a Guggenheim Best Idea, he said.

"We continue to believe ORCL is a decade stock, driven by the massive (and profitable) opportunity ahead for AI training and inferencing," said DiFucci. He also cited the traditional Public Cloud business and additional AI-enabled cloud database and cloud applications for his bullish stance. DiFucci expects all the factors to drive revenue and profit acceleration over the next few years.

While Oracle delivered 850 MW of data center capacity in Q1 FY27, Bond cautioned that Infrastructure as a Service revenue in any quarter depends more on the timing of when that capacity comes online, than on the headline MW number. DiFucci expects IaaS growth to stay strong, with full-year growth expected above Q1 FY27's 120% constant currency rate. Management expects total revenue growth to accelerate sequentially throughout fiscal 2027, as the faster-growing infrastructure business makes up a larger share of the mix.

DiFucci addressed concerns regarding customer concentration, saying OpenAI's share of total remaining performance obligations has dropped from 80% last June to about 50% now, and is expected to keep falling as new contracts are signed with new and existing customers.

DiFucci ranks No. 263 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 62% of the time, delivering an average return of about 17.5%. See Oracle Insider Trading Activity on TipRanks.

Space systems and launch services provider Rocket Lab (RKLB) was recently initiated as a buy with an $80 price target by Raymond James analyst Brian Gesuale.

"RKLB has assembled launch, spacecraft, components, payloads and optical communications, with Neutron and Iridium completing medium-lift and applications/spectrum in 2027," said Gesuale.

The 5-star analyst highlighted that with the completion of the Iridium acquisition, Rocket Lab will become the only vertically integrated space company with positive free cash flow (FCF). The deal will add a deployed constellation valued at more than $3 billion, globally coordinated L-band spectrum and about $500 million in earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation, and amortization (EBITDA) and $300 million in free cash flow (FCF).

Gesuale highlighted RKLB's strong revenue and backlog growth and progress toward positive EBITDA and FCF in 2027/2028. Rocket Lab is moving from a heavy investment phase into monetization, with estimated revenue growth of 59% in 2026, the analyst said, noting that RKLB's backlog has more than doubled to almost $2.4 billion from $1.1 billion in Q3 2025.

Given strong growth projections, the analyst expects RKLB's gross margins to expand from about a 35% trough in 2027 to about 47% by 2030, while R&D spending is estimated to decline from ~35% of sales in 2026 to about 10% in 2028, creating substantial operating leverage as the platform matures.

Gesuale ranks No. 369 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of about 17%. See Rocket Lab Statistics on TipRanks.

J.P. Morgan analyst Doug Anmuth recently upgraded Meta Platforms (META) to buy from hold and raised his price target to $820 from $640. Despite the year-to-date underperformance of META, the 5-star analyst still sees significant upside, as Meta is in the early stages of launching frontier models and AI-powered offerings beyond advertising, particularly the Muse AI agent and Meta Model API access.

"Frontier models are at the core of Meta's product and monetization pipeline over a multi-year period, and its path toward superintelligence," said Anmuth.

In the summer of 2025, Meta set a goal to rebuild its Meta Superintelligence Labs team and deliver frontier AI models within a year, the analyst said. The team appears to have met its goal, rapidly advancing from the rollout of Muse Spark 1.1 in July to Muse Spark 1.3, which is competitive with Claude and GPT models, Anmuth said.

Meanwhile, Meta's Watermelon product will expand opportunities in consumer products, business intelligence, and Family of Apps engagement, the analyst said. The product will enhance internal operations and overall efficiency, said Anmuth, who's confident of Meta's ability to bring consumer-driven AI products to its extensive user base of 4 billion and views its distribution network as a competitive advantage.

While Meta has focused on frontier models and AI product development, Anmuth also sees solid opportunity in its AI infrastructure capacity, which he believes will support future compute needs.

Anmuth ranks No. 832 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 57% of the time, delivering an average return of about 9.40%. See Meta Platforms Financials on TipRanks.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 2:00 p.m. EDT Share

The Federal Open Market Committee approved the following statement for release by a 10 â 2 vote:

The Committee decided to maintain the target range for the federal funds rate at 3-3/4 to 4 percent, in support of the Federal Reserve's dual mandate. The Committee is continuing its policy of maintaining ample reserves in the banking system.

Economic activity is expanding at a strong pace. While uncertainty remains elevated owing, in part, to geopolitical developments, domestic spending has been resilient. Productivity growth is strong, and capital investment is robust. Job gains have kept pace with the workforce, and the unemployment rate has changed little.

Inflation remains elevated. The accumulation of more information will help the Committee determine the policy stance appropriate to ensure a timely return of inflation to the Committee's 2 percent goal. The Committee will deliver price stability.

Voting against the monetary policy action were Lisa D. Cook and Beth M. Hammack, who preferred to raise the target range for the federal funds rate by 1/4 percentage point at this meeting.

#2881. Austin Reaves remains out vs. Jazz, Walker Kessler returns

Following a dramatic overtime win against the Hornets on Tuesday, the Lakers will still be at nearly full strength against the Jazz on the second night of a back-to-back, with one major exception.

Austin Reaves will miss his third game since suffering a heel bruise against the Bucks last Wednesday. However, he is the only player on the injury report for the purple and gold with Walker Kessler, who also missed Tuesday's game, set to return.

There are multiple players notable in their exclusion from this injury report. First, as noted, Kessler will return after being out with leg soreness on Tuesday.

"Walker, some lower leg soreness, got bumped," head coach JJ Redick said before Tuesday's game. "Just thinking big picture. We don't think it's serious, but it makes sense for him to, as a precautionary, be out tonight."

Luka Dončić playing both legs of the back-to-back is notable, too. Luka's two missed games this season have both been during a back-to-back, but he did play in the team's previous back-to-back at home.

As for Austin, Redick said Tuesday that he would do on-court work Wednesday morning to see where he's at. The injury and return remain a pain tolerance matter with Redick having previously said that there is no structural damage with the injury.

It is easier to be patient with Reaves' return with the Lakers winning. But with a match-up with the Spurs looming, it would also be nice to see him return soon so he can take part in that contest.

As for the Jazz, who are also on the second night of a back-to-back, their injury report is nearly the same as it was against the Clippers. Jaxson Hayes, who was out on Tuesday, has been upgraded to questionable.

Jaren Jackson, Lauri Markkanen and Jusuf Nurkic are all out. Kobe Bufkin also will not be making his return to LA as he is with Utah's two-way team.

#2871. Top Wall Street analysts like these 3 dividend stocks for steady income

Stock markets remain volatile as investors weigh uncertainty in the Middle East, rising interest rates and concerns about the artificial intelligence (AI) trade.

Given this volatile backdrop, investors seeking a stable income stream can enhance their portfolios with the addition of dividend stocks. In this regard, tracking recommendations of top Wall Street analysts can help shortlist attractive dividend stocks that are backed by strong financials.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Integrated oil and gas giant Chevron (CVX) is the first dividend pick. Earlier this month, the company paid a quarterly dividend of $1.78 per share. At an annualized dividend of $7.12 per share, CVX offers a dividend yield of about 3.5%.

After a meeting with senior leadership, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on CVX and raised his price target to $240 from $225. Among the key takeaways Mehta emphasized was that Chevron is building a solid pipeline of promising international exploration projects across Latin America, the Middle East, West Africa and the Eastern Mediterranean. In particular, he mentioned the focus on Venezuela, where Chevron expects to more than double gross production to 600 kbd from about 280 kbd by 2031, using three joint ventures.

Mehta also noted Chevron's efforts to deploy new technologies, especially in shale and tight-oil operations. CVX is focused on boosting productivity and efficiency to maximize free cash flow, he added.

Initiatives such as artificial lift optimization, AI and machine learning, and advanced chemical treatments are expected to improve recovery by about 10% in new wells. Consequently, capital spending per barrel in U.S. shale is projected to fall 25% in 2026, with total Permian spending dropping below $3.5 billion.

Mehta also said that Chevron sees power as a growth opportunity and is using its U.S. natural gas portfolio, key equipment and partnerships to meet rising demand. Notably, Chevron recently signed a 20-year power purchase deal with Microsoft (MSFT) to supply 2.67 GW of behind-the-meter capacity. This project, called Kilby, is expected to see its first power delivery in 2028 and generate mid-teens returns and long-term cash flows.

Mehta ranks No. 536 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 61% of the time, delivering an average return of 11.3%. See Chevron Statistics on TipRanks.

Enterprise Products Partners (EPD) is a master limited partnership (MLP) that provides midstream energy services to producers and consumers of natural gas, natural gas liquids (NGLs), crude oil, refined products and petrochemicals. With a quarterly cash distribution of 56 cents per common unit ($2.24 per common unit annualized), EPD offers a yield of about 6%.

Following a recent call with the company, RBC Capital analyst Elvira Scotto reiterated a buy rating on EPD with a price target of $42. The analyst slightly lowered her estimates for the second half of 2026, citing margin and volume normalization, though demand dynamics remain strong.

While the 5-star analyst expects a sequential decline due to normal seasonality and normalization following high spreads in the first half of the year, she still expects EPD to report another solid quarter (Q3 2026) and enter 2027 with a favorable setup. Ahead of Q3 earnings, Scotto expects investors to focus on export demand durability, EPD's ability to profit from spot cargo sales and growth in Permian volumes.

Scotto continues to expect $150 million of common unit buybacks per quarter in the second half of 2026. She expects buybacks to increase to $200 million per quarter in 2027, as capex spending declines and free cash flow improves.

In total, Scotto is bullish on EPD and views it as a core MLP holding that has both offensive and defensive characteristics. The analyst praised EPD's diverse asset base as ensuring stable cash flows. "Furthermore, the partnership's multi-year organic growth backlog helps provide visibility on long-term distribution growth," Scotto said.

Scotto ranks No. 199 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been successful 67% of the time, delivering an average return of 15.9%. See Enterprise Products Partners Financials on TipRanks.

Brookfield Infrastructure Partners (BIP) operates a diversified portfolio of high-quality utilities, transport, midstream and data assets. The company declared a quarterly distribution of 45.5 cents per unit, payable on September 29. At an annualized distribution of $1.82, BIP offers a yield of 5.2%.

Recently, BMO Capital analyst Devin Dodge reaffirmed a buy rating on Brookfield Infrastructure with a price target of $47. The analyst cited improved visibility into BIP's growth and views its corporate simplification as a potential catalyst.

"Layering in the benefits from the proposed corporate simplification, a multiple well below historical levels and an attractive yield, we believe BIP offers a compelling risk/reward and it remains a preferred idea," said Dodge.

With more organic growth projects in the pipeline and new partnership investment opportunities, Dodge believes that BIP has clearer visibility into where it will deploy capital. That should support continued double-digit growth in funds from operations (FFO) per unit.

Dodge said the semiconductor foundries that BIP is developing with Intel (INTC) are expected to be fully commissioned by the end of 2026, with returns improving through Q4 2026. Dodge expects the joint venture to boost FFO by 300-400 basis points.

Dodge ranks No. 476 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 68% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.5%. See Brookfield Infrastructure Partners KPIs on TipRanks.

#2870. Warriors always were a Steph Curry injury away, and now must avoid catastrophe

Warriors always were a Steph Curry injury away, and now must avoid catastrophe originally appeared on NBC Sports Bay Area

In the nicest, least degrading way possible, Warriors coach Steve Kerr gave his version of "it is what it is" to reporters Wednesday night in Dallas after his team's fourth straight blowout loss.

The Warriors extended their losing streak to four without Steph Curry on this five-game road trip, falling 116-94 against the Dallas Mavericks in a game where they trailed by as many as 29 points. They've lost by an average of 27 points the last three games and were down by 31 and 43 points the previous two.

That's the difference Curry still makes. It's also the difference of being down over $150 million in salaries to injuries within the first month of the 2026-27 NBA season.

What was obvious going into the campaign is as evident as ever: The Warriors always were a Curry injury away.

"This is just where we are right now," Kerr said to reporters at American Airlines Center. "We're wiped out with injuries. The guys are really, really competing and playing hard and staying together, and that's all we can do.

"Things are going to turn, but we've got to give ourselves the best chance to help them turn."

Sometimes, it really is as simple as that.

Golden State entered the season with $89.3 million on its shelf between Jimmy Butler ($56.8 million), Kristaps Porziņģis ($20 million) and Moses Moody ($12.5 million). Add Curry's $62.6 million, and you're now over $150 million.

And that doesn't even count Brandon Williams, whose missing firepower has been a big part of the Warriors' drowning offense.

Since Wednesday was the first night of a back-to-back, that meant 40-year-old center Al Horford also was subjected to street clothes.

The same reason fans felt such hope during the Warriors' eight-game win streak is the same reason there's such angst in this four-game losing streak, which stems from the same frustrations in the offseason.

Surround Curry with smart, competent players and your team has a chance. Take away Curry from a group of mainly role players and your chances are bare to none.

Yaxel Lendeborg's skyrocket ascension took away so much stress in the non-Curry minutes, and even in the few games the Warriors' aging star needed to rest at the start of the season.

He scored 26 points in his first game without Curry, which was just the third game of Lendeborg's NBA career, and led a valiant effort in a road loss to the Phoenix Suns on the second night of a back-to-back. Lendeborg then went on a 37-point rampage in front of his home fans to beat the LA Clippers in Curry's second rest game.

Like the entire Warriors, Lendeborg was shellshocked when Curry left against the Houston Rockets one week ago, but responded admirably in the second quarter and finished with 25 points.

Brandin Podziemski scored 22 in that loss to the Rockets. In the three games since, Lendeborg and Podziemski mostly have been letdowns as lead options offensively.

They scored a combined 23 points – 13 for Lendeborg and 10 for Podziemski – in their latest blowout loss. They've averaged a combined 26 points in the last three games while shooting 30.6 percent from the field and 22.6 from 3-point range.

"I think a lot of guys are really trying to do some things on their own," Kerr said. "Yaxel had probably four or five off the dribble fadeaway shots in the first half, just trying to carry us. He knows that we need him, and he's trying really hard. BP as well.

"We're attempting some difficult shots. We've got to get the ball moving and create easier shots for one another."

Warriors players are trying to temper expectations on Lendeborg. The ultra-skilled forward thrived playing off Curry's gravity, and already was relied upon to do more than most rookies are expected from the opening tip.

He's an older rookie, yes. He also hasn't played a full month of an NBA regular season yet.

Draft evaluators didn't see Lendeborg as a No. 1 scoring option after excelling as the ultimate offensive complement at Michigan, and he's being thrust into the fire without perhaps the most skilled offensive player in basketball history.

Podziemski has to play closer to who he was the final six weeks of last season when the Warriors didn't have Curry and Butler. Fans feel they have more reason to chastise him since signing a $110 million contract extension, but these past few games also have been a reminder that first and second options sign for more than what Podziemski did.

A Curry-less Warriors team often is a Draymond Green-less Warriors team, too. Green, who returned on a one-year, $27.7 million contract, is giving them nothing on offense. Only one of the two can be a solo act.

The shining light in Dallas was Will Richard. Playing through a torn left UCL in his thumb, Richard scored 20 points in 27 minutes off the bench. He bounced all around the court, swiped four steals and got up to throw down some emotionally-charged dunks.

Richard played with an appropriate amount of desperation that his Warriors teammates need to match.

"The Steph injury is unfortunate," Georges Niang said. "We're missing bodies. But at the end of the day, I think we have good spirit. … It's just adapting to the players that we have. Now that Steph is not out there, it's different lineups that we weren't used to playing. I think we're getting closer than what it looks like.

"I feel corny saying that, but it's going to turn."

The first few weeks of the Warriors' season have been a resurrection from an 0-3 start, riding the highs of an eight-game win streak and now crashing back through bumpy waters in a current four-game losing streak.

Butler's injury ended the Warriors last season in January. These Warriors always were a Curry injury away from catastrophe, and they'll have to right the ship quickly to not spin out of control waiting for his return in the coming weeks.

#2861. Media firebrand asks if Black players will let Lakers’ ‘white brothers’ win it all

Stephen A. Smith had a question for Black NBA players on Wednesday morning after the Lakers won their ninth straight game.

"These white brothers can play, they can play. They can win a championship. This is the NBA!" Smith said on "First Take." "Y'all gonna let them win a championship?"

Los Angeles Lakers forward Ziaire Williams, Luka Doncic, Jake LaRavia and Walker Kessler wait during a time-out in Phoenix, Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026. AP Photo/Darryl Webb

Smith was talking about a Lakers team built around Luka Doncic, Austin Reaves and Walker Kessler.

A week earlier, he had picked Los Angeles to bow out by the second round, saying on Mad Dog Sports Radio that he had never seen a team win a title with three white players as its best players.

He still doubts the Lakers will win it all, but he and fellow ESPN analyst Chiney Ogwumike agree they can.

Smith framed the question around race. Ogwumike made the stronger case for the Lakers by pointing to what Doncic did with the game on the line. He trusted Quentin Grimes to take the biggest shot of the night.

Luka Doncic reacts to making a bad pass during the second half against the Charlotte Hornets in Los Angeles, Tuesday, Nov. 17, 2026. Getty Images

But Smith made the question about race. Ogwumike pointed to something that says more about the Lakers' title chances. With the game on the line, Luka Doncic trusted Quentin Grimes to take the biggest shot of the night.

The final possession of regulation in Tuesday's 118-114 overtime win over Charlotte gave her a reason to believe. With Reaves and Kessler sidelined, the Lakers had rallied from 18 down and needed a 3 to stay alive. Doncic had 36 points and a single defender in front of him. Grimes had his hands up.

Quentin Grimes of the Los Angeles Lakers reacts after scoring during a game against the Charlotte Hornets in Los Angeles, Tuesday, Nov. 17, 2026. Getty Images

Doncic found him. Grimes buried the tying 3 with 1.7 seconds left. It was his 10th of the night, making him and Kobe Bryant the only Lakers to hit that many in a game. Grimes finished with a career-high 47.

"It's the vibe. It's the ability to say, 'Hey, we have enough to pursue a championship,'" Ogwumike said on ESPN. "Not gonna lie, when I heard that, I was like, I'm not sure about that."

Where Smith raised the question of race, Ogwumike saw what could make the Lakers champions: chemistry developing between the Lakers' best player and a new addition attempting to make his mark.

"That doesn't start in that moment," she said. "That starts in training camp."

#2858. Federal Reserve Board announces pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, and changes to streamline and adjust their check services

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 1:30 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Thursday announced pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, such as the clearing of checks, automated clearing house (ACH) transactions, instant payments, and wholesale payment and settlement services.

By law, the Federal Reserve must establish fees to recover the costs, including imputed costs, of providing payment services over the long run. The Federal Reserve expects to recover 101.5 percent of actual and imputed expenses in 2027, including the return on equity that would have been earned if a private-sector firm provided the services. Overall, price changes for 2027 will result in an estimated 1.4 percent average price increase for established, mature services.

In addition, the Federal Reserve Banks announced changes to streamline and adjust their check services. As described in the request for information on check services issued last year, the volume of checks processed by the Federal Reserve Banks is declining, but checks remain an important payment instrument for many consumers and businesses. The changes were informed by public feedback and ensure that the Federal Reserve Banks continue to provide check services efficiently. The adjustments include optimizing check processing windows during low-volume periods, increasing fees that banks and credit unions pay to process checks, and other operational updates. These changes do not impact consumers' and businesses' ability to pay with or receive checks and only impact how banks and credit unions process checks with Federal Reserve Banks.

The entire 2027 fee schedule for all of the Federal Reserve's payment services is published on FRBservices.org®. Additional information on the Federal Reserve's payments services, including service usage statistics, is available on the Board's website.

For media inquiries, please email [email protected] or call 202-452-2955.â¯â¯

Board of Governors of the Federal Reserve System

#2855. How LeBron James' move from Lakers to 76ers unlocked both teams' superpowers

The NBA standings, heading into games scheduled for Wednesday, Nov. 18, tell a remarkable story involving LeBron James.

The Los Angeles Lakers, the team James left before the 2026-27 season, are 13-2 and off to their best start since 2008-09 (14-1 to start that season). That season, the Lakers won the NBA title.

The Philadelphia 76ers, the team James joined before this season, are 12-3 and off to their best start since 2000-01. That season, the 76ers reached the NBA Finals and lost to…the Lakers.

"You never know what… change can totally affect your team,'' said Randy Pfund, part of the Lakers coaching staff from 1985 to 1994 and general manager of the Miami Heat from 1995 to 2008.

Of course the oddity here is, four weeks into the season, the team James left and the team he joined both appear to have gotten better.

Analytics can help explain how the Lakers are thriving without James. Especially as presented by Vangelis Sarlis, a Greek postdoctoral researcher who has mined NBA data.

"I would describe it as role redistribution rather than addition by subtraction,'' Vangelis Sarlis told USA TODAY Sports. "…The more persuasive analytical explanation is that LeBron's departure clarified roles.''

With James no longer playing for the Lakers, Luka Dončić leads the league in scoring with 38.8 points per game and twice has scored more than 50 points.

"Luka Dončić is now the unquestioned primary creator and Austin Reaves the secondary creator, with personnel around them designed for spacing, screening and finishing,'' said Sarlis, whose research team includes George Papageorgiou and professor Christos Tjortjis at International Hellenic University in Thermi, Greece.

Their work includes the 2024 academic paper titled "Leveraging Sports Analytics and Association Rule Mining to Uncover Recovery and Economic Impacts in NBA Basketball."

Through 14 games, Sarlis pointed out that the Lakers' offensive rating had risen from 118.2 last season to about 123 points per 100 possessions, the best in the league. He also cited NBA tracking that shows the Lakers have experienced the league's biggest jump in transition scoring, from 22.6 to 26.2 points per game.

"That supports the observation that the offense looks quicker and more fluid,'' Sarlis said. He also noted Dončić's usage rate of 40.2%, which leads the league in adding to the Lakers' offense: "It's faster, but it is more concentrated, not less.''

Then there was James' $52 million salary with the Lakers.

The funds once spent on him allowed the Lakers to re-sign Reeves and overhaul the roster. Key additions include center Walker Kessler, who's averaging 14.5 points per game and 10.9 rebounds per game; guard Quentin Grimes, who is averaging 12.1 points per game and scored 47 points in the Lakers' 118-114 overtime victory against the Charlotte Hornets Tuesday, Nov. 17; and Sandro Mamukelashvili and Collin Sexton, both notable contributors.

"That personnel is…the most concrete link to LeBron's departure,'' Sarlis said.

LeBron James is not the same player who won four NBA titles, including one with the Lakers during the COVID-shortened season in 2020.

In 11 games played this season, James' shooting percentage (.443) is the worst since his rookie year and his 3-point shooting percentage (.225) is the worst of his career. But Bob Whitsitt, former general manager of the Seattle Supersonics and Portland Trail Blazers, suggested the early-season shooting trouble obscures James' contributions.

"He's always been a good passer, but maybe right now his passing is a little bit more of what's needed in Philly than the other things he's doing,'' Whitsitt said.

James leads the 76ers with 6.9 assists per game. No one seems to be overly concerned he's averaging only 15.1 points on a team with potent scorers like Tyrese Maxey, Jaylen Brown and Joel Embiid.

"Rather than expecting LeBron to remain the primary scorer, the 76ers can utilize him as a fourth scoring option, playmaker and facilitator alongside Embiid, Maxey, and Brown,'' said Sarlis, the Greek researcher. "His experience and basketball intelligence could help optimize team rotations and strengthen Philadelphia's pursuit of another championship.''

Pfund won two NBA championship rings as a member of the Lakers' coaching staff from 1985 to 1994. He won a third NBA championship ring as the general manager of the Miami Heat from 1995 to 2008.

He also spent most of his NBA career working for legendary Pat Riley.

"Pat always believed you could never have too many veteran guys who would come through in the clutch,'' Pfund said.

Sarlis said his team's research shows James, 41, is among the most consistent historical performers in decisive moment.

Clutch veterans are most valuable in the playoffs, said Pfund, who cited two examples.

In February 1987, when Pfund was an assistant on the Lakers' coaching staff, Lakers traded for center Mychal Thompson. He was was an 8th-year NBA veteran. Not only did he prove to be a capable backup center behind Kareem Abdul-Jabbar, he also played a role in the Lakers' winning the 1987 NBA title.

Then in 2005, when Pfund was the general manager of the Heat, the team at Riley's behest traded for Antoine Walker. He was an 11th year veteran and three-time All Star with the Boston Celtics.

"He was pretty average all year,'' Pfund said. "And people were (saying), man, they spent too much money and whatever.''

But the veteran player delivered in the postseason., according Pfund.

"Antoine Walker had made a lot of big baskets,'' Pfund said. "He didn't hesitate at all.''

The Heat beat the Dallas Mavericks in the 2006 NBA title, winning the franchise's first NBA title.

It's still five weeks away from the NBA's slate of Christmas Day games, which this year will include -- you guessed it -- the 76ers vs. the Lakers at Crypto.com Arena in Los Angeles.

But Whitsitt, the former NBA general manager, allowed himself to think well beyond Christmas.

"What kind of story would it be if the Lakers played the Sixers in the Finals?'' he said.

It's an enticing possibility as Whitsitt talked about what he expects to happen next. James will continue to blend in with his new teammates. The Lakers will eventually tighten up on defense. Hopefully, both teams will stay healthy as the season unfolds.

"They're both fun to watch,'' Whitsitt said.

This article originally appeared on USA TODAY: How LeBron James' move from Lakers to 76ers unlocked both teams' superpowers

#2854. Agencies extend comment period for proposed third-party risk management guidance

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:15 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation, the Federal Reserve Board, the National Credit Union Administration, and the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency today announced that they will extend, until December 16, the comment period for proposed guidance on managing risks associated with third-party relationships. Comments on the proposal were originally due November 16.

The proposed guidance reflects the agencies' supervisory experience and lessons learned from examining financial institutions' third-party risk management practices. It is intended to help banks and credit unions tailor their risk management practices to the risks associated with individual third-party relationships. The proposed guidance takes a principles-based approach and, like all supervisory guidance, is non-binding.

When finalized, the federal bank regulatory agencies plan to rescind existing third-party risk management guidance and replace it with the finalized guidance to promote consistency and prudent innovation in the banking industry. NCUA also plans to rescind its existing third-party risk management guidance and will conduct its own reviews to update related guidance as needed.

Also today, the Federal Reserve Board separately announced that it will extend, until December 16, the comment period on a proposed third-party risk management guide specifically for Federal Reserve-supervised traditional community banks.

Federal Register notice: Proposed Third-Party Risk Management Guidance; Extension of Comment Period

Federal Register notice: Proposed Third-Party Risk Management Guide for Traditional Community Banking Organizations

Agencies seek comment on proposed third-party risk management guidance and issue statement on community bank engagement with core service providers

Board of Governors of the Federal Reserve System

#2852. NFL blasts Mike Florio’s ‘inappropriate’ Mike Vrabel-Hitler joke

The NFL isn't happy with Mike Florio's apparent Adolf Hitler joke.

During Tuesday's episode of "ProFootballTalk Live" after the Patriots' ugly loss to the Lions in Germany, Florio and his co-host Chris Simms laughed back and forth about the New England head coach's facial hair, with the latter saying he had a "German mustache."

Florio, an NFL insider for NBC Sports, went along with the gag.

New England Patriots head coach Mike Vrabel gestures during a press conference after an NFL football game in Munich, Germany, Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026. AP Photo/Matthias Schrader

"You gotta be careful trimming that thing," Florio said. "…If you're in Germany, you gotta be careful trimming the sides of that thing."

Florio, who never mentioned Hitler by name, doubled down after apparently getting a reaction out of the show's producer.

"Gary's groaning," Florio said. "It's funny, Gary. It was 80 years ago. Let us have some fun, for crying out loud."

The exchange didn't go over nearly as well at the league's headquarters.

"We found the remarks inappropriate and offensive," the NFL's executive vice president of communications and public affairs Jeff Miller said in a statement to The Post.

A league source told The Post that the NFL took its concerns about Florio's remarks directly to NBC Sports, one of its longtime broadcast partners.

Asked about potential disciplinary actions, the source said the league had "expressed displeasure with his remarks."

Mike Florio stands on the field before an NFL game between the Baltimore Ravens and Kansas City Chiefs in Kansas City, Mo., Thursday, Sept. 5, 2024. Icon Sportswire via Getty Images

Florio and Simms responded to the backlash on Wednesday.

"On yesterday's 'PFT Live' show, we made comments that some found offensive and we apologize," they said in a statement posted by NBC Sports.

Florio also serves as an insider on the network's "Sunday Night Football" pregame show, "Football Night in America," a role he has held since 2010.

Sunday's game at Allianz Arena in Munich, a 35-10 Lions victory, marked the sixth NFL regular-season contest played in Germany.

The NFL has played at least one regular-season game in Germany every year since 2022.

#2851. Top Wall Street analysts are upbeat about the prospects for these 3 stocks

September ended on a mixed note, with the S&P 500 and Dow Jones Industrial Average falling 0.5% and 4.3%, respectively, while the Nasdaq Composite climbed 1.9%. Markets remained on edge with higher oil prices and Treasury yields weighing on investor sentiment and triggering fears of additional interest rate hikes.

In this climate, investors with a long-term horizon can track recommendations from top Wall Street analysts to find stocks with solid growth prospects.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Neocloud company CoreWeave (CRWV) is benefiting from robust AI-driven demand for cloud computing.

Recently, JPMorgan analyst Samik Chatterjee upgraded CoreWeave to buy from hold, raising his price target to $125 from $120. The analyst cited favorable pricing, driven by solid demand, for the upgrade.

Chatterjee said CoreWeave is now more willing to sign premium priced, shorter-term contracts, which lift his revenue and margin estimates. CRWV stock performance has been underwhelming as investors worry about high capital spending, the analyst noted. That said, he believes that higher prices and increased margins will more than offset the rise in debt to fund capital spending, improving the stock's valuation.

Chatterjee noted the 25% price hike in July across CoreWeave's product offerings and frequent price increases by rivals like Nebius (NBIS), adding that some peers are charging nearly three times more for short-term compute contracts than what CoreWeave charges on longer-term deals.

"CoreWeave's leverage of the opportunity [is] evident in its recent press release highlighting contracts signed in F3Q at ~$40M/MW," said Chatterjee. JPMorgan said higher pricing is already contributing 5-10 percentage points to contract margins versus earlier deals.

Chatterjee ranks No. 4 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 75% of the time, delivering an average return of 55.3%. See CoreWeave Statistics on TipRanks.

Palo Alto Networks (PANW) is a provider of cybersecurity solutions across network, cloud, security operations, AI and identity. Following a discussion with management, BTIG analyst Gray Powell reiterated a buy rating on PANW and raised his price target to $425 from $404.

After a review of Palo Alto's product-level growth catalysts, Powell sees clear upside to both revenue and next-generation security (NGS) annual recurring revenue (ARR) in fiscal 2027. He expects PANW to deliver pro forma revenue growth of more than 17%, above the consensus estimate of 14.6%, and NGS ARR growth of more than 26% versus the Street's expectation of 24.5%.

Over the past five years, the Palo Alto business mix, has moved to higher-growth product areas and new markets. from what was earlier viewed as a hardware-centric network security company, Powell said. The 5-star analyst estimates that hardware firewalls and attached subscriptions now account for less than 50% of revenue.

Meanwhile, high-growth solutions, including Prisma SASE, software firewalls, Cortex XSIAM, Chronosphere and Idira now represent more than 35% of PANW's revenue. "Investors may not fully realize the growth potential within PANW's platform story," Powell said.

BTIG is optimistic about the prospects of Chronosphere in observability, CyberArk in identity and software firewalls and Prisma AIRS in the network and AI security business.

Powell ranks No. 320 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 61% of the time, delivering an average return of 17.3%. See Palo Alto Networks KPIs on TipRanks.

In a research note on September 30, Rosenblatt analyst Scott Devitt reiterated a buy rating on e-commerce and cloud computing platform Amazon (AMZN), raising his price target to $360 from $335. Fears that agentic commerce poses a major threat to Amazon's advertising business seem "overstated," he said.

Devitt noted that the growing adoption of personal agents and AI shopping assistants has sparked concern about displacement of Amazon's retail media business. But the 5-star analyst remains bullish, given Amazon's ability to adapt to changing consumer behavior, as it's done in the past.

Additionally, Devitt argues that even if an AI agent helps a shopper discover a product, Amazon can still handle the purchase and delivery, keeping much of the transaction value. He highlighted that management believes that existing Amazon shoppers will likely continue to start their shopping journey on the platform because it has better personalization, accurate pricing and inventory information that third-party agents still lack.

Overall, Devitt expects Amazon's retail business to prove a net beneficiary of agentic commerce. Fewer clicks would not hurt the company if the value of each remaining click and overall volumes keep rising, he said. The argument is that even if Amazon loses some sponsored ad revenue over the next few years, it would likely be only a small part of the company's total business and could be offset by growth in other areas.

"Consistent with our AI framework, we continue to view Amazon as one of the most resilient leaders in our coverage, combining owned compute infrastructure with a physical fulfillment moat," said Devitt. His bullish view is supported, Devitt said, by expectations that Amazon Web Services (AWS) will exit the year at a more than 45% growth rate with continued margin improvement.

Devitt ranks No. 765 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 49% of the time, delivering an average return of 12%. See Amazon Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

#2846. Hapless Chargers tickets now cheaper than a stadium cheeseburger

A SoFi Stadium cheeseburger now costs more than tickets to watch the lowly Los Angeles Chargers play.

Astute sports fans noticed resale tickets for the Chargers-New York Jets game on Sunday going for as low as $14 at one point on TickPick, less than the $16.50 in-stadium cost for a cheeseburger.

Whether that says something about the hapless Chargers, who are 1-8 this season, or the cost of burgers, is up to fans.

Whether that says something about the hapless Chargers, who are 1-8 this season, or whether it suggests the burgers are expensive, is up to fans. X/@@ArashMarkazi

But there's no question the Nov. 22 game is fairly inconsequential to the NFL playoff picture as both teams sit firmly out of the race for a Super Bowl title. X/@@ArashMarkazi

Most online are embarrassed about their team and complaining about the expensive nature of going to a game outside of admission — the cheapest resale tickets on other sites like StubHub were just under $30.

Fans gather at SoFi Stadium during a timeout in an NFL game between the Los Angeles Chargers and the Los Angeles Rams in Inglewood, Calif., Nov. 1, 2026. Icon Sportswire via Getty Images

"Chargers should return back to San Diego," one person wrote.

"Don't forget parking $150," another added.

But there's no question the Nov. 22 game is fairly inconsequential to the NFL playoff picture as both teams sit firmly out of the race for a Super Bowl title.

The 2-8 Jets have lost five straight games, including their last two bouts by 19 and 23 points, after a 2-3 start. Starting quarterback Geno Smith is also dealing with an ankle injury, though he expects to play Sunday, he told ESPN.

An ultra wide-angle view shows the field prior to an NFL game between the Arizona Cardinals and the Los Angeles Chargers at SoFi Stadium in Inglewood, Calif. Icon Sportswire via Getty Images

A SoFi Stadium cheeseburger now costs more than tickets to watch the lowly Los Angeles Chargers play. Getty Images

There is an unusual amount of optimism surrounding head coach Jim Harbaugh's team after they upset the Baltimore Ravens on the road Monday night — finally breaking speculation that they could go on to be the NFL's first winless team over a full season since the 2017 Cleveland Browns after starting 0-8.

Fans have booed the team's horrific start, which comes in part due to paltry quarterback play from starter Justin Herbert. Herbert has thrown just six touchdowns in nine games with 11 picks. No matter what happens in the second half of the season, the Chargers will underperform their 2025 record of 11-6.

The Los Angeles Rams, who share the stadium with the Chargers, have seen substantially more success on the field. The 6-4 Rams are just a game out of a Wild Card spot with the help of a lockdown secondary and veteran quarterback Matthew Stafford.

Therefore, their ticket prices are much, much more expensive. Two standing-room-only tickets for their Thanksgiving Eve game against the Green Bay Packers cost more than $500 total on TickPick.

#2845. What we learned as Darius Acuff Jr.‘s 36 aren't enough in Kings' loss to Suns

What we learned as Darius Acuff Jr.'s 36 aren't enough in Kings' loss to Suns originally appeared on NBC Sports Bay Area

SACRAMENTO – The Kings continue to make their dismal start to the 2026-27 NBA season even worse.

They lost Wednesday night to the Phoenix Suns, 128-107, falling to 1-13 on the year and extending their franchise-record home winless streak to begin the season to nine games.

The defeat capped their longest home stand of the season at Golden 1 Center at 0-7, with many members of the lifeless 13,016 in attendance leaving early.

The game followed a familiar pattern. It was close at halftime – Phoenix led by five at the break – the Suns pulled away and opened a 21-point lead in the third quarter, and the Kings were unable to pull off the unlikely comeback while a handful of fans boo'd at the final buzzer.

Their 1-13 start ties the worst start to a season in franchise history.

Another pattern continued as prized rookie Darius Acuff Jr. showed off his scoring chops, finishing with a game-high 36 points and six assists.

The 2026 NBA Draft's No. 7 overall pick got off to a hot start, scoring 10 points in the first quarter and adding 11 more in the second quarter. His 21 first-half points were the second-most of any half this season (he scored 22 on Oct. 23 against Portland). He made 7 of 10 shots including 4-of-7 from 3-point range and all of his 2-point attempts.

Acuff continued his torrid scoring with 13 of the Kings' 24 points in the third quarter while Phoenix's lead ballooned. He finished 11 of 22 from the floor including 6 of 11 from deep. He made 8 of his 10 free throws (no other King shot more than two).

Acuff finished two points shy of his career-high 38 he scored on Halloween against the Memphis Grizzlies when he made nine threes. He entered the night leading rookies in total points, 30-point games, assists and made threes.

He got little help Wednesday. Zach LaVine was second on the Kings with 18 points. Keegan Murray (12) was Sacramento's only other player in double digits.

Acuff hit a 3-pointer midway through the third quarter against Dillon Brooks and immediately started talking trash down the court. It pushed Acuff's total 26 points.

Brooks responded with a step-back three just over a minute later, giving him 25, and leading to a timeout. Brooks was animated and yelled things towards fans seated near the visiting bench.

Brooks tied for the Suns lead with 29 points while Phoenix outscored Sacramento by 26 points during his 31 minutes. Brooks led the team with five made 3-pointers. Devin Booker matched with 29. The two combined to shoot 22 of 36 and (61 percent).

Brooks has been a known irritant throughout his career. He often got into it with former Kings guard/forward DeMar DeRozan and appears to be continuing his Sacramento beef with Acuff.

The Kings scored the first six points of the fourth quarter with three straight baskets from Keegan Murray, who had only four points in the first three quarters combined. It trimmed a 15-point lead to nine and injected some much-needed life back into Golden 1 Center.

But the Suns responded with a Jalen Green dunk out of a timeout and pushed the lead back to double-digits. Phoenix went on to outscore the Kings 29-23 in the first half and 63-47 in the second half.

Domantas Sabonis sat the entire fourth quarter while the Suns went small, using Miles Bridges as their center late. Sabonis sat despite having eight points, 13 rebounds and eight assists.

Jeffries, D-N.Y., still needs to be officially elected when the new Congress convenes in January. Jeffries would make history as the first Black speaker of the House if he is elected in January.

#2840. 49ers spare no expense for Mexico City trip with hope of taking team to new heights

Kyle Shanahan gets to decide what he believes will best prepare the 49ers to play football.

That arrangement is particularly appreciated this week, with the 49ers setting up shop in Colorado Springs. Colo., to acclimate to elevation before facing the Vikings on Sunday in Mexico City.

San Francisco 49ers head coach Kyle Shanahan watches the first half of a game against the Dallas Cowboys in Arlington, Texas, Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026. IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

Moving an NFL operation for a week requires more than plane tickets and hotel rooms. Especially when the goal is to make a temporary home feel as much like the real thing as possible.

The 49ers brought the people, equipment and resources necessary to keep their routine intact. That includes the staff responsible for feeding them.

At some point, someone has to approve the expense.

Shanahan appreciates that his bosses have consistently approached those decisions by asking what helps the team, giving him the freedom to make recommendations based on football.

"That's been pretty amazing, because it's always just, 'What do you guys think helps you for football?'" Shanahan said. "And that's what I always try to think about."

That trust takes a concern off the coach's plate when weighing how to prepare for an unusual trip.

"No one's ever said, 'Yeah, but try to go this way because one's 100 times more than the other," Shanahan said.

There is plenty for Shanahan to consider before taking his team to another country for a game. The Vikings still require a full week of study. Players still need treatment, meetings and practice. Every hour spent sorting through a disruption is time that could have gone toward getting ready for Sunday.

The players appreciate the commitment, too, even if Christian McCaffrey is reserving judgment on how much practicing at elevation will help Sunday.

He has no such uncertainty about the organization's willingness to give its players every opportunity to prepare.

"One thing I know about our organization, and that I really respect, everybody from the top down, is they'll do anything to try to help us get acclimated to whatever adversities or things that we have to face throughout the week," McCaffrey said. "So if we're going to play at altitude, to come here and getting acclimated is part of that. So, you know, I appreciate it."

For the players and coaches, the investment shows up in the details. The meals remain familiar. The tools needed to study opponents, lift weights and recover from injuries travel with them.

Christian McCaffrey of the San Francisco 49ers leaves the field after a game against the Dallas Cowboys on Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026, in Arlington, Texas. Getty Images

"When we do travel, we do it pretty right. Like, we bring our cooks, we bring everything," Shanahan said. "Our offices look the same when we get to our hotel rooms. There's not one video thing that's not here. We bring equipment for all the strength and conditioning stuff, the rehab stuff."

The objective is to give the 49ers the benefits of preparing at elevation while preserving the infrastructure that supports a normal workweek.

That takes planning well beyond the practice field. Bringing the equipment is one part of it. Having the people who know how each player uses it, and what he needs to get through the week, helps make the arrangement work. The familiarity matters when so much else about the trip is different.

It also gives players fewer logistical concerns as they work through their football responsibilities.

San Francisco 49ers linebacker Fred Warner (54) reacts against the Dallas Cowboys at AT&T Stadium in Arlington, Texas, Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026. IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

Shanahan's appreciation also comes with perspective from conversations with peers and his experience elsewhere.

"Even talking to some other coaches who have traveled before and done stuff like this, I just think it's a little bit different how our organization does it," Shanahan said. "Just with how they do whatever they think is right for the players and to give us an advantage, anything we need.

"I'm not saying the places I've been at and did it bad by any means, but if I started here first, I probably would have thought that."

For Shanahan, the willingness to fund all those details makes an expensive week away feel like a worthwhile investment in preparation for Sunday.

#2839. Game Preview #16: Wolves vs Knicks Game Thread

Following Thursday's drunken back-and-forth beautiful mess of a come-from-behind win, it's time for Karl-Anthony Towns and the NBA Champion New York Knicks to come to town for their first matchup of the season. Despite winning seven of their last eight games, this will be the Wolves' third game in four nights after having a very physical back-to-back against the Portland Trail Blazers.

Meanwhile, the Knicks have won six of their last eight games and will now travel to Minnesota at the vert tail end of a four-game road trip. They most recently played Tuesday night in Toronto where they took care of a Kawhi Leonard-less Raptors team in the clutch.

It is Karl-Anthony Towns that deserves first billing in this matchup. In his previous two visits to Target Center since the trade, KAT is averaging 36 points, 16.5 rebounds, 3.5 assists, and is a +37 in all his minutes in the opposing team's uniform in Minneapolis.

At an 8-6 overall record, don't let the Knicks middling record fool you into thinking this team is just cruising through the regular season with a championship hangover. Since their 0-3 start, the Knicks are 8-3 with all of their losses in that time coming within single digits. In those 11 games, they are the third ranked team in net rating, trailing just the San Antonio Spurs and Boston Celtics. They are traveling to Minneapolis with a fully healthy squad and will be looking to stack more wins to climb out of the mire of the middle of the Eastern Conference.

Who: Minnesota Timberwolves (11-4) vs New York Knicks (8-6)

Where: Target Center, Minneapolis Minnesota

Key Strength Stat: Points in the paint (5th)

Key Weakness Stat: Assist Percentage (28th)

Spider-Man Pointing Meme Stat: Second Chance Points (3rd)

Key Strength Stat: Offensive Rebounding Percentage (1st)

Spider-Man Pointing Meme Stat: Second Chance Points (1st)

Over the last ten games, the Wolves are second in the league in points in the paint thanks in large part to Ayo Dosunmo's increased usage and desire to win a footrace to the rim each time he catches the ball. The Knicks are the third best team at defending the paint thanks to a core of very versatile wing defenders who are able to switch on everything and stay in prime position defensively. Couple their paint defense with being the best rebounding team in the league and it is clear where their money is made on the defensive end.

They want to force you to hit shots from midrange and beyond, and then grab rebounds with their long and athletic wings.

The Wolves are no slouches themselves with the sixth best rebounding percentage. They utilize a very similar front court when they are fully healthy. The Knicks play at the slowest pace in the league and utilize a half-court offense that is going to feature a lot of Jalen Brunson trying to get to the rim and draw (or at least make it look like he's drawn) contact. The Wolves defend well without fouling (5th best opponent free throw rate), but the NBA Finals MVP always has a few tricks up his sleeve that could get Wolves defenders in foul trouble.

If things are mucked up in the paint like the above numbers suggest, the importance of the Wolves making the "one extra pass" to an open shooter and converting could well be the secret sauce to unlocking offense against a very versatile defensive team. Isolation basketball that leans too heavily into the Wolves' 28th ranked assisting percentage will absolutely make this game feel like the Wolves are pushing a boulder uphill all night long.

The Knicks starting group of Jalen Brunson, Mikal Bridges, Josh Hart, OG Anunoby and KAT is a rare starting group that is as versatile as the Wolves. If Kuminga can't go and Minnesota chooses to put Jaden McDaniels on Jalen Brunson, there are going to be a lot of matchup issues for the Wolves. Assuming LaMelo Ball takes Josh Hart, Dosunmo will shift to Bridges, leaving OG Anunoby with a tantalizing matchup with either a size advantage on Cody Williams, or a speed and athleticism advantage on Trey "Elite Rim Protector" Lyles.

While Dosunmo could be on Brunson duty instead McDaniels, the defensive scheme makes a whole lot more sense if Kuminga is good to go.

My prediction is all based on the X-Factor of Jonathan Kuminga's availability Thursday. If he plays, the Wolves win a physical back-and-forth defensive slug fest 106-102 where Kuminga can match physicality with OG Anunoby.

If he's out, the Wolves just won't have quite enough bodies to throw at the trio of Brunson, Bridges, and Anunoby, losing 116-106. Wait for Kuminga's game status to be announced then go place your bets as the outcome has already been foretold!

#2838. Junk bonds are 'flashing yellow.' Watch these warning signs

Cracks are forming in the junk bond market as investors demand higher payouts for owning the market's riskiest debt. It isn't time to ditch high-yield bonds, but investors should pay attention to the warning signs.

High-yield bonds now yield 8.1%, up from 7.22% a month ago. The increase reflects a jump in yields across the curve as investors bake in more inflation from high energy prices and other pressures, including concern about the deficit — hitting nearly $2 trillion in the fiscal year that ended Sept. 30.

The high-yield market is also showing stress on the credit side with spreads recently widening to levels not seen since April, according to the Federal Reserve Bank of St. Louis. Credit spreads are the difference in yield between the bonds and Treasurys of similar maturities. Wider spreads mean investors are demanding higher yields for holding corporate debt, viewing it as riskier.

Spreads are at 315 basis points in the overall high-yield market, higher than a year ago but still below levels in March when they reached 346 bps. One basis point equals one one-hundredth of a percent, or 0.01%.

The high-yield market consists of bonds rated BB+ by S&P and Fitch and Ba1 and under by Moody's. The lowest-rated cohort, CCC and below, has seen the most movement with spreads climbing dramatically over the past year to roughly 1,250 bps.

Right now, the high-yield market is "flashing yellow" but is "far from red," said Michael Arone, chief investment strategist at State Street Investment Management.

It makes sense that investors are demanding more compensation for taking on additional credit risk as borrowing costs rise, he said.

Yields are elevated across the board with the 10-year Treasury reaching its highest level since 2002 earlier in the week.

"The bigger question is whether this is simply a repricing of interest rate risk, or the beginning of a more fundamental reassessment of credit quality," Arone said.

He's in a wait-and-see mode since earnings are still growing, interest-coverage ratios remain good, and while default rates have ticked up some, he believes it is not concerning.

That said, the starting point in spreads is likely weighing on investors' psyche, since they are still low by historical standards.

"There's a small margin of error here, which I also think raises the anxiety level," Arone explained. "The compensation that investors are receiving for taking on this credit risk isn't overwhelming relative to history, and therefore subtle changes in credit spreads can be concerning."

While there may be concerns about part of the lower-rated market, the overall high-yield market is in good fundamental shape.

In fact, credit quality is at a record high, with BB bonds making up over 60% of the market compared with 38% prior to the global financial crisis, said Kelley Gerrity, a fixed income strategist at Morgan Stanley Investment Management.

"We've had higher-quality companies coming in, and with higher rates now, you also have more discipline from companies that are more indebted ... just because of the higher cost of capital, so that actually is creating a bit of a healthier picture as well," she said.

The lowest tier of high yield has always been more speculative, since the bonds have a higher default risk. Therefore, it's no surprise that they are the ones seeing the most widening in spreads, said Collin Martin, head of fixed income research and strategy at the Schwab Center for Financial Research.

The overall move in high-yield spreads have been "orderly," he said. "Are there cracks forming? I think the cracks are logical cracks with the lowest-rated bonds and it's too early to say that it's spreading to the broad credit market."

In addition, fluctuations within the CCC-rated and lower spreads have been idiosyncratic, Gerrity added. Morgan Stanley recently broke down the spreads in the lowest cohort into two buckets: performing assets and non-performing assets, which are defined as spreads above 1,000 bps.

The non-performing spread to worst is 2,818 bps, while the performing bucket — the biggest piece of the CCC market — is 461 bps, she said.

"It is isolated," she said. "I'm not necessarily sure that that will go away anytime soon, but it doesn't tell the story of overall concern and flashing caution for credit markets right now."

For now, investors should remain selective within higher yield, Gerrity advised.

"We're looking for the best relative value opportunities, and we think that exists within that single-B cohort, and so we're leaning into that," she said.

Investors should be concerned if there is a steep widening in the broader high-yield market.

But there's scant evidence of stress in the BB cohort. Spreads in that group are at 194 bps, up from 179 bps a year ago — although the move hasn't been in a straight line.

"We're going to be focusing our attention on what are considered some stronger businesses," Martin said. "If we start to see the markets demand higher spreads there also, that's what we'll be looking at to see if risks are really rising."

It's also important to understand that the Federal Reserve is raising rates in a strong economic environment, said R.J. Gallo, chief investment officer of global fixed income at Federated Hermes. The central bank is concerned about an inflation problem amid high fossil fuel prices due to the Iran war, while growth has been stronger than expected, he noted.

"With growth being good as the reason that the Fed is hiking, then you wouldn't expect high yield to blow out because growth means revenues stay up, means cash flow and profitability stays up," he explained.

High yield becomes a disaster when the economy is heading into a sharp economic downturn, Gallo said.

"That's when spreads really widen out," he said. "But a recession is not the odds-on bet. Now, talk to me in six months if the Fed keeps hiking over and over, if oil prices stay high for longer, well, then maybe we will start to wonder."

#2837. Nvidia’s Kress faces a divided workforce as some employees push for Huang’s return

The day before Colette Kress led her first earnings call as Nvidia's interim CEO, she did not want to talk about her predecessor, Jensen Huang.

"This is a new era for Nvidia," she told employees in an internal meeting on Monday, according to a person present, who spoke on the condition of anonymity. When someone brought up Huang, Kress quickly changed the subject, according to the person.

On Wednesday, Kress addressed the whole company at the first Nvidia all-hands meeting since the public chaos over Huang's affair with an alleged Chinese spy and other reported relationships. And while Kress will be tasked with filling the leadership vacuum created by Huang's departure, she is also tamping down public calls to bring him back.

Huang himself weighed in with his first public statement Tuesday saying he would be "honored" to return to Nvidia.

Kress, who has served as the company's CFO since 2013, must steady employees and convince customers and investors that Nvidia can execute on its ambitious targets without the man who made them.

At Wednesday's meeting with staffers, Kress was joined by newly appointed board chair Harvey Jones as well as Ian Buck, who oversees Nvidia's hyperscale and high performance computing business, according to an attendee.

Kress' message was, "Let's move forward," said the person, who asked not to be named because the event was private.

Nvidia's leaders have made clear that the board does not plan to bring Huang back.

"The board has determined that they will not reinstate Jensen," Kress said in an interview with CNBC's Jim Cramer on Tuesday. "We want to grow in terms of everything that we need to do with our employees, our customers, our shareholders, and that is where we are going to focus moving forward."

Jones echoed the sentiment on Tuesday's earnings call. "The Nvidia board unanimously accepted his resignation and does not intend to reinstate Jensen as CEO," he said in opening remarks.

Nvidia declined to comment for this story.

Kress stressed on the earnings call that the company was focused on the workers who remain, who she said are "committed to doing their life's work at Nvidia." The company also reported better-than-expected earnings and seemed to calm investors' nerves, at least for the moment.

Some employees are telling a different story. Support for Huang's return is growing inside Nvidia, sources tell CNBC. An online petition circulating since the weekend has nearly 200,000 signatures, with more than 10,000 of them claiming to be current Nvidia employees. Nvidia currently has roughly 42,000 employees.

Outside Nvidia, Huang's reinstatement has been called for by at least two major investors and tech heavyweights such as Elon Musk, Y Combinator CEO Garry Tan and Epic Games CEO Tim Sweeney. President Donald Trump said he "would not mind" seeing Huang return as CEO.

Several employees posted on X over the last few days, voicing support for Huang and calling for his return. One robotics engineer said the founder's departure led to his own decision to leave.

Another senior Nvidia employee, Nader Khalil, sent a letter to the company's board and top executives warning that failing to reinstate Huang could lead to a loss of "irreplaceable talent," according to The Wall Street Journal.

Huang was known for his deep day-to-day involvement in his company's operations, often responding directly to engineers and chiming in personally on technical projects.

Engineering employees, in particular, were "extremely devoted to him, almost like a demigod," according to a former Nvidia employee who was at the company for more than 15 years and spoke to CNBC on condition of anonymity.

Wednesday's all-hands is a standard meeting: Nvidia traditionally holds one on the morning after each quarterly earnings call, according to the former employee.

Still, the meeting is unlike anything the company has experienced. Employees told CNBC that they plan to share their grievances at the all-hands, though it wasn't clear ahead of time if they would be allowed to ask questions.

Huang took time to address employee questions during previous post-earnings all-hands meetings, the former Nvidia employee told CNBC, adding that Kress is "eager to demonstrate there's a new sheriff in town."

Kress ended Tuesday's earnings call with an upbeat message: "Our customers and our partners are with us. And employees are committed. We've got this."

— CNBC's Lora Kolodny contributed to this report.
